 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	# Component-sensitive line concentration and the square-root conditional regime
     2	
     3	## Abstract
     4	
     5	For the affine line-versus-point test on $\mathbb F_p^2$, I retain the line-support measure in the Kominers--Thaler--Zheng sparsification argument. If a connected accepted-incidence subgraph has minimum degree $k$ and its line side occupies a fraction $\tau$ of all affine lines, then a reaching family of size $O(\tau p^2/k)$ suffices. The resulting trivariate explainer has weighted degree $D=O(\sqrt{d\tau p^2/k})$. Component-local accounting shows that the proof closes under
     6	
     7	$$
     8	k^3\gg d\tau p^2\quad\text{and}\quad k^2\gg dp,
     9	$$
    10	
    11	and yields one total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial with agreement $\Omega(k/p)$. Thus a standard pruned component with $k=\Theta(\epsilon p)$ and $\tau=O(\epsilon)$ admits the conditional square-root threshold $\epsilon=\Omega((d/p)^{1/2})$, with global agreement $\Omega(\epsilon)$.
    12	
    13	This does not improve the unconditional $1/3$ benchmark: no argument here forces the small-component hypothesis. Moreover, a balanced-color construction using constant line polynomials proves that a single family which covers half of every accepted line can genuinely require $\Omega(p/\epsilon)$ lines. Hence a further improvement must be a sparsify-or-decode argument, not a uniformly smaller graph-theoretic cover.
    14	
    15	## Test and Notation
    16	
    17	Fix a prime $p$ and an integer $100<d<p$; throughout $m=2$. There are $p(p+1)$ affine lines, each containing $p$ points, and every point lies on $p+1$ lines. Sampling a uniform line and then a uniform point on it is therefore the same as sampling a uniform incident pair.
    18	
    19	Let $f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p$, and let every affine line $L$ carry a polynomial $P_L$ of degree at most $d$. Put
    20	
    21	$$
    22	\epsilon=\Pr_{L,\,x\in L}[P_L(x)=f(x)].
    23	$$
    24	
    25	The accepted-incidence graph has an edge $(x,L)$ exactly when $x\in L$ and $P_L(x)=f(x)$. For a subgraph $G=(X,T,E)$, write $k=\operatorname{mindeg}(G)$, $t=|T|$, and
    26	
    27	$$
    28	\tau=\frac{t}{p(p+1)}.
    29	$$
    30	
    31	All graph degrees below are accepted degrees, not ambient incidence degrees.
    32	
    33	## Prior Results
    34	
    35	The active corpus contained no completed submission or verifier audit at the start and end of this run; every active verified, promising, rejected, and bottleneck leaderboard was `[]`. This was checked against [the durable manifest](/Users/kaizheng/Documents/ChatGPT/Line-vs-Point/research_state/DATA_MANIFEST.json) and both campaign databases, excluding every directory named `superseded`.
    36	
    37	The quoted benchmark is Theorem 1.1 of [Kominers--Thaler--Zheng, ECCC TR26-147, revision 1](https://eccc.weizmann.ac.il/report/2026/147/revision/1/download): over every finite field, $\epsilon\ge C(d/p)^{1/3}$ implies a single total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial with agreement at least $c\epsilon$.
    38	
    39	The explanation of the cubic exponent is my reconstruction from their proof, not a quoted theorem: Lemma 3.2 gives $|R|=O(p/\epsilon)$; Lemmas 4.1--4.3 give $D=O(\sqrt{d|R|})=O(\sqrt{dp/\epsilon})$; and identity extension and later lifting require $D=O(\epsilon p)$. Squaring gives $d p/\epsilon=O(\epsilon^2p^2)$, or $\epsilon^3=\Omega(d/p)$.
    40	
    41	For context, Theorem 4.2 of [Harsha--Kumar--Saptharishi--Sudan](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.12752v1) displays the condition $p>C d/\epsilon^7$ for its weak bivariate best-fit-oracle theorem. Calling this an exponent-$1/7$ threshold is an algebraic rewrite of that displayed condition. It is not used below.
    42	
    43	## Theorem
    44	
    45	**Theorem A (proved component-sensitive decoder).** Let $C_*=2^{40}$. Suppose $G=(X,T,E)$ is a connected subgraph of the accepted-incidence graph with minimum degree $k$ and line measure $\tau$. If
    46	
    47	$$
    48	k^2\ge C_*dp,\qquad k^3\ge C_*d\tau p^2,
    49	$$
    50	
    51	then there is $Q\in\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that
    52	
    53	$$
    54	\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge \frac{k}{800p}.
    55	$$
    56	
    57	The constant is deliberately unoptimized.
    58	
    59	**Corollary B (conditional exponent $1/2$).** Let $\epsilon$ be the test agreement and let $G_0$ be a pruned graph supplied by KTZ Lemma 3.1, so $k_0=\lfloor\epsilon p/100\rfloor$. Suppose some connected component of $G_0$ has line measure at most $\Lambda\epsilon$, where $\Lambda\ge1$. Define
    60	
    61	$$
    62	C_\Lambda=\sqrt{8{,}000{,}000\,C_*\Lambda}.
    63	$$
    64	
    65	If $\epsilon\ge C_\Lambda(d/p)^{1/2}$, then some total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial $Q$ satisfies
    66	
    67	$$
    68	\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge\frac{\epsilon}{160000}.
    69	$$
    70	
    71	This is conditional only in the campaign sense: the implication is proved, but the extra small-component premise is not known for every table.
    72	
    73	**Proposition C (proved global-cover obstruction).** Let $p\ge103$ be prime and let $2\le K\le p/(100\log p)$. For every campaign degree $100<d<p$, there is a valid table using constant line polynomials for which
    74	
    75	$$
    76	\frac1{2K}\le\epsilon\le\frac2K,
    77	$$
    78	
    79	all accepted point and line degrees are within a factor two of their means, and every set $R$ which reaches at least half of the accepted points of every affine line satisfies
    80	
    81	$$
    82	|R|\ge\frac{Kp}{16}\ge\frac{p}{32\epsilon}.
    83	$$
    84	
    85	Nevertheless, one constant global polynomial agrees with $f$ on at least $\epsilon/2$ of the points.
    86	
    87	## Proof or Conditional Proof
    88	
    89	**[P1] (proved: component size).** Apply KTZ Lemma 2.1 to the point and line sides of $G$. Its exact mixing error is $\sqrt{\mu(X)\mu(T)}/\sqrt{p+1}$. The first hypothesis and $d>100$ imply $1/\sqrt{p+1}\le k/(4p)$. The argument of KTZ Lemma 6.2 therefore gives
    90	
    91	$$
    92	\mu(X),\tau\ge\frac{k}{4p}.
    93	$$
    94	
    95	In particular, $t\ge k(p+1)/4$.
    96	
    97	**[P2] (proved: component-sensitive reaching family).** Select each line of $T$ independently with probability $8/k$. For $x\in N_G(L)$, at least $k-1$ other accepted lines pass through $x$, so $x$ is reached with probability at least $3/4$. For distinct points of a fixed affine line $L$, the relevant sets of other lines are disjoint: two such points have only $L$ as a common line. The reach indicators are consequently independent. Chernoff gives failure probability at most $e^{-\deg_G(L)/24}\le e^{-k/24}$ for a fixed $L$. The hypotheses imply $k\gg\log p$, so a union bound over fewer than $2p^2$ lines succeeds. A second Chernoff bound controls the sample size. Hence one choice $R$ has, with $r=|R|$,
    98	
    99	$$
   100	\frac{4t}{k}\le r\le\frac{16t}{k},
   101	$$
   102	
   103	and reaches more than half of $N_G(L)$ for every $L\in T$. By [P1], $r\ge p+1$.
   104	
   105	**[P3] (proved: interpolation).** Set
   106	
   107	$$
   108	D=16\bigl(\lfloor\sqrt{dr}\rfloor+1\bigr).
   109	$$
   110	
   111	Then $D^2>256dr$ and
   112	
   113	$$
   114	D^2\le1024dr\le\frac{16384dt}{k}.
   115	$$
   116	
   117	Using $t=\tau p(p+1)\le2\tau p^2$ and $k^3\ge C_*d\tau p^2$ gives $D/k<1/5000$. Thus $D<k\le p$, while $re p>D$.
   118	
   119	KTZ Lemma 2.2 supplies at least $D^3/(64d)$ eligible monomials of weighted degree at most $D$. Each identity $A(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$ costs at most $D+1$ homogeneous conditions, and $D^2>256dr$ makes the number of variables exceed $r(D+1)$. A nonzero interpolant exists. Because $r>D$, it cannot be independent of $Z$; the allowed $Z$-exponents make $A_Z\ne0$. Choose one of minimum weighted degree. KTZ Lemma 2.3 makes it squarefree.
   120	
   121	**[P4] (proved: extension).** If $x$ is an accepted point of $L$ reached by $M\in R\setminus\{L\}$, then $P_L(x)=f(x)=P_M(x)$ and hence $A(x,P_L(x))=0$. More than $k/2>D$ distinct such points lie on every $L\in T$. Since $A(L(t),P_L(t))$ has degree at most $D$, it vanishes identically. No Markov loss occurs here.
   122	
   123	**[P5] (proved: characteristic-safe cleanup).** Write $A=HB$, where $H(X,Y)$ is the gcd of the coefficients of $A$ as a polynomial in $Z$. At most $D$ lines make $H|_L$ identically zero. On every other line, exact division in the domain $\mathbb F_p[t]$ gives $B(L(t),P_L(t))\equiv0$. The squarefree $B$ has degree below $p$. KTZ Lemma 2.4 supplies a formal directional derivative $\mathcal D$ with $\gcd(B,\mathcal DB)=1$. KTZ Lemma 2.6, via the resultant, bounds lines on whose polynomial graph both vanish by $D(D-1)$. Every other line has at most $D$ accepted points where $(\mathcal DB)(x,f(x))=0$. Thus at most
   124	
   125	$$
   126	pD^2+Dt
   127	$$
   128	
   129	accepted edges are deleted.
   130	
   131	**[P6] (proved: local deletion and pruning).** Since $|E|\ge kt$,
   132	
   133	$$
   134	\frac{pD^2+Dt}{|E|}\le\frac{D}{k}+\frac{16384dp}{k^2}<\frac1{4000}.
   135	$$
   136	
   137	Put $k_1=\lfloor k/100\rfloor$. Iteratively deleting vertices of current degree below $k_1$ removes fewer than
   138	
   139	$$
   140	k_1(|X|+t)\le 2(k_1/k)|E|\le |E|/50
   141	$$
   142	
   143	additional edges. A nonempty graph of minimum degree $k_1$ remains, although it may fragment.
   144	
   145	**[P7] (proved: simple roots).** At every remaining point choose two incident lines; $k_1\ge2$. Their directions are independent in $\mathbb F_p^2$. Differentiating their formal identities, if $B_Z(x,f(x))=0$, forces $B_X(x,f(x))=B_Y(x,f(x))=0$. This contradicts the retained condition $(\mathcal DB)(x,f(x))\ne0$. Hence every retained value is a simple $Z$-root. No derivative is divided by in this step.
   146	
   147	**[P8] (proved: lift, propagate, and measure).** Choose any connected component of the graph left by [P6]. Its minimum degree is $k_1>D$. KTZ Lemmas 2.7 and 6.1 lift the line roots through one point to a single total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial $Q$. Simple-root uniqueness propagates the same $Q$ through the component, as in KTZ Lemma 6.3; there is no factor-count loss. The first numerical hypothesis also gives $1/\sqrt{p+1}\le k_1/(4p)$. KTZ Lemma 6.2 then gives point measure at least
   148	
   149	$$
   150	\frac{k_1}{4p}\ge\frac{k}{800p},
   151	$$
   152	
   153	proving Theorem A.
   154	
   155	**[P9] (conditional: translate to local agreement).** In Corollary B, the stated threshold makes $k_0\ge\epsilon p/200$. The inequality $\epsilon^2p\ge8{,}000{,}000C_*\Lambda d$ implies both
   156	
   157	$$
   158	k_0^2\ge C_*dp,
   159	\qquad
   160	k_0^3\ge C_*d(\Lambda\epsilon)p^2.
   161	$$
   162	
   163	Theorem A therefore gives agreement at least $k_0/(800p)\ge\epsilon/160000$. This is the exponent-$1/2$ calculation. Its only unresolved campaign premise is the existence of the component with $\tau\le\Lambda\epsilon$.
   164	
   165	**[P10] (proved: realizable balanced-color graph).** Independently color every point and every affine line uniformly from $[K]$, inject the colors into $\mathbb F_p$, put $f(x)$ equal to the point color, and make $P_L$ the corresponding constant line color. Conditional on a vertex color, its accepted degree is binomial with mean $p/K$ on the line side or $(p+1)/K$ on the point side. A Chernoff bound followed by a union bound over fewer than $3p^2$ vertices has failure probability at most $6p^2e^{-p/(12K)}<1$. Therefore a deterministic realization with the claimed degree bounds exists. These constant line polynomials are degree at most the fixed campaign degree $d>100$; this is not an analysis of the excluded case $d=0$.
   166	
   167	**[P11] (proved: cover lower bound).** Let $S$ be the points incident to an accepted edge from $R$. The line-degree upper bound gives $|S|\le2p|R|/K$. If every line has at least half its accepted points in $S$, summing over all $p(p+1)$ lines gives at least $p^2(p+1)/(4K)$ accepted incidences from $S$. The point-degree upper bound is $2(p+1)/K$, so $|S|\ge p^2/8$. Hence $|R|\ge Kp/16$. Since $\epsilon\ge1/(2K)$, this is at least $p/(32\epsilon)$. Finally, $f$ takes only $K$ values, so one constant has agreement at least $1/K\ge\epsilon/2$.
   168	
   169	## Exponent Ledger
   170	
   171	| Stage | Input | Output/loss |
   172	|---|---|---|
   173	| KTZ reconstruction | $|R|=O(p/\epsilon)$ | $D=O(\sqrt{dp/\epsilon})$; $D<\epsilon p$ gives $\epsilon^3\gtrsim d/p$ |
   174	| Component sampling | $t=\tau p(p+1)$, degree $k$ | $|R|=\Theta(t/k)=\Theta(\tau p^2/k)$; Chernoff/union losses are constant |
   175	| Interpolation | $r=|R|$ | $D=\Theta(\sqrt{dr})=O(\sqrt{d\tau p^2/k})$ |
   176	| Extension | root supply $k$ | $D<k$ requires $k^3\gtrsim d\tau p^2$ |
   177	| Degenerate deletion | bad edges $pD^2+Dt$ | relative loss $O(dp/k^2+D/k)$, requiring $k^2\gtrsim dp$ |
   178	| Mixing | minimum degree $k_1$ | $p^{-1/2}\lesssim k_1/p$; absorbed because $d>100$ and $k^2\gg dp$ |
   179	| Global agreement | surviving component | $\Pr[Q=f]\ge k/(800p)$; no power loss |
   180	| Small-component corollary | $k=\Theta(\epsilon p)$, $\tau=O(\epsilon)$ | both constraints become $\epsilon^2\gtrsim d/p$ |
   181	| Global-cover obstruction | $\epsilon=\Theta(1/K)$ | every uniform cover has $|R|=\Omega(Kp)=\Omega(p/\epsilon)$ |
   182	
   183	## Counterexample Attempts
   184	
   185	The balanced-color table proves that the $p/\epsilon$ global-cover scale is not merely an artifact of independent sampling. It is an actual degree-at-most-$d$ line-versus-point table. It does not refute soundness, because a constant decoder already has agreement $\Omega(\epsilon)$.
   186	
   187	Concentrating all good lines in a subset of directions also does not refute the conditional result. With at least two good directions, the accepted graph is connected, has line measure $\tau=\epsilon$, and is governed by the planted polynomial.
   188	
   189	Independent random tables accept with probability about $1/p$, far below the campaign range. Attempts using $t^p-t$ or other inseparable functional aliases require line degree at least $p$ and are excluded by $d<p$.
   190	
   191	At the lower boundary $d=101$, the square-root scale decreases from about $0.9902$ at $p=103$ to $0.1005$ at $p=10007$. When $d$ approaches $p$, both cube-root and square-root scales approach one, and the large-constant hypotheses become vacuous. No near-$p$ counterexample is claimed.
   192	
   193	## Characteristic Audit
   194	
   195	The proof is entirely over the prime field and keeps $D<p$. Formal and functional identities cannot be confused for line polynomials because $d<p$. The eligible-monomial restriction ensures $A_Z\ne0$. Squarefreeness and perfectness rule out an inseparable irreducible factor with all partial derivatives zero. The generic derivative is chosen over $\mathbb F_p$ using fewer than $p$ exceptional hyperplanes. Resultants, rather than discriminant division, control common line roots. Newton lifting divides only by a certified nonzero simple-root derivative. Ordinary interpolation is used with no hidden multiplicities or Hasse-derivative assumptions.
   196	
   197	## Limitations
   198	
   199	The result does not prove that every accepted core contains a component with $\tau=O(\epsilon)$. A single connected component may have $\tau$ close to one, in which case the new parameter law returns the cubic benchmark. The explicit constant $2^{40}$ is safe but intentionally very loose, making the finite nonvacuous range much smaller than the exponent notation suggests. Proposition C obstructs only a single cover required to work for every line; it does not obstruct an algebraically informed decoder or an adaptive localization argument. Finite computations above are falsification checks only and play no role in the asymptotic proof.

Writing k for component minimum degree and tau for its fraction of all affine lines, the two necessary budgets are k^3 >= C d tau p^2 (identity extension) and k^2 >= C d p (degenerate-line deletion).","With k=Theta(epsilon p), these become epsilon^3 >= C tau d/p and epsilon^2 >= C d/p. Its m=2, q=p specialization is epsilon>=C(d/p)^(1/3) with one total-degree-at-most-d polynomial having agreement at least c epsilon.","version":"ECCC TR26-147, revision 1, 16 August 2026"},{"hypotheses":"Bivariate affine incidence; weighted degree (1,1,d); explainer degree D<p; simple-root lifting.","paper":"Scott Duke Kominers, Justin Thaler, and Kai Zhe Zheng, Improved Soundness for the Line--versus--Point Test","result_id":"Lemmas 2.1--2.7, 3.1--3.2, 4.1--4.3, 5.1--5.2, and 6.1--6.3","used_for":"Point-line mixing, benchmark pruning, monomial count, squarefree and directional-derivative cleanup, resultant bound, Newton lifting, and component propagation. The tau-sensitive size calculation and component-local deletion ledger are proved in this note and are not quoted from KTZ.","version":"ECCC TR26-147, revision 1, 16 August 2026"},{"hypotheses":"Best-fit degree-d line polynomial on each line and q>C d/epsilon^7.","paper":"Prahladh Harsha, Mrinal Kumar, Ramprasad Saptharishi, and Madhu Sudan, An Improved Line-Point Low-Degree Test","result_id":"Theorem 4.2","used_for":"Historical bivariate comparison only. The displayed q>C d/epsilon^7 condition is quoted; exponent 1/7 is its algebraic rewrite and is not used in the proof.","version":"ECCC TR23-182 / arXiv:2311.12752 v1, 21 November 2023"}],"next_tasks":["Prove an unconditional sparsify-or-decode dichotomy: either some accepted core component has tau=O(epsilon), or the labels in a larger component already determine a global degree-d decoder.","Investigate algebraic rank among line-graph constraints. The balanced-color obstruction defeats smaller graph covers but has very low label rank and an immediate constant decoder.","Search for a connected, nearly biregular, degree-d-realizable acceptance component with tau much larger than epsilon and no Omega(epsilon)-agreeing polynomial; such an example would directly test the remaining structural premise."],"note_markdown":"# Component-sensitive line concentration and the square-root conditional regime\n\n## Abstract\n\nFor the affine line-versus-point test on $\\mathbb F_p^2$, I retain the line-support measure in the Kominers--Thaler--Zheng sparsification argument. If a connected accepted-incidence subgraph has minimum degree $k$ and its line side occupies a fraction $\\tau$ of all affine lines, then a reaching family of size $O(\\tau p^2/k)$ suffices. The resulting trivariate explainer has weighted degree $D=O(\\sqrt{d\\tau p^2/k})$. Component-local accounting shows that the proof closes under\n\n$$\nk^3\\gg d\\tau p^2\\quad\\text{and}\\quad k^2\\gg dp,\n$$\n\nand yields one total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial with agreement $\\Omega(k/p)$. Thus a standard pruned component with $k=\\Theta(\\epsilon p)$ and $\\tau=O(\\epsilon)$ admits the conditional square-root threshold $\\epsilon=\\Omega((d/p)^{1/2})$, with global agreement $\\Omega(\\epsilon)$.\n\nThis does not improve the unconditional $1/3$ benchmark: no argument here forces the small-component hypothesis. Moreover, a balanced-color construction using constant line polynomials proves that a single family which covers half of every accepted line can genuinely require $\\Omega(p/\\epsilon)$ lines. Hence a further improvement must be a sparsify-or-decode argument, not a uniformly smaller graph-theoretic cover.\n\n## Test and Notation\n\nFix a prime $p$ and an integer $100<d<p$; throughout $m=2$. There are $p(p+1)$ affine lines, each containing $p$ points, and every point lies on $p+1$ lines. Sampling a uniform line and then a uniform point on it is therefore the same as sampling a uniform incident pair.\n\nLet $f:\\mathbb F_p^2\\to\\mathbb F_p$, and let every affine line $L$ carry a polynomial $P_L$ of degree at most $d$. Put\n\n$$\n\\epsilon=\\Pr_{L,\\,x\\in L}[P_L(x)=f(x)].\n$$\n\nThe accepted-incidence graph has an edge $(x,L)$ exactly when $x\\in L$ and $P_L(x)=f(x)$. For a subgraph $G=(X,T,E)$, write $k=\\operatorname{mindeg}(G)$, $t=|T|$, and\n\n$$\n\\tau=\\frac{t}{p(p+1)}.\n$$\n\nAll graph degrees below are accepted degrees, not ambient incidence degrees.\n\n## Prior Results\n\nThe active corpus contained no completed submission or verifier audit at the start and end of this run; every active verified, promising, rejected, and bottleneck leaderboard was `[]`. This was checked against [the durable manifest](/Users/kaizheng/Documents/ChatGPT/Line-vs-Point/research_state/DATA_MANIFEST.json) and both campaign databases, excluding every directory named `superseded`.\n\nThe quoted benchmark is Theorem 1.1 of [Kominers--Thaler--Zheng, ECCC TR26-147, revision 1](https://eccc.weizmann.ac.il/report/2026/147/revision/1/download): over every finite field, $\\epsilon\\ge C(d/p)^{1/3}$ implies a single total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial with agreement at least $c\\epsilon$.\n\nThe explanation of the cubic exponent is my reconstruction from their proof, not a quoted theorem: Lemma 3.2 gives $|R|=O(p/\\epsilon)$; Lemmas 4.1--4.3 give $D=O(\\sqrt{d|R|})=O(\\sqrt{dp/\\epsilon})$; and identity extension and later lifting require $D=O(\\epsilon p)$. Squaring gives $d p/\\epsilon=O(\\epsilon^2p^2)$, or $\\epsilon^3=\\Omega(d/p)$.\n\nFor context, Theorem 4.2 of [Harsha--Kumar--Saptharishi--Sudan](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.12752v1) displays the condition $p>C d/\\epsilon^7$ for its weak bivariate best-fit-oracle theorem. Calling this an exponent-$1/7$ threshold is an algebraic rewrite of that displayed condition. It is not used below.\n\n## Theorem\n\n**Theorem A (proved component-sensitive decoder).** Let $C_*=2^{40}$. Suppose $G=(X,T,E)$ is a connected subgraph of the accepted-incidence graph with minimum degree $k$ and line measure $\\tau$. If\n\n$$\nk^2\\ge C_*dp,\\qquad k^3\\ge C_*d\\tau p^2,\n$$\n\nthen there is $Q\\in\\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that\n\n$$\n\\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]\\ge \\frac{k}{800p}.\n$$\n\nThe constant is deliberately unoptimized.\n\n**Corollary B (conditional exponent $1/2$).** Let $\\epsilon$ be the test agreement and let $G_0$ be a pruned graph supplied by KTZ Lemma 3.1, so $k_0=\\lfloor\\epsilon p/100\\rfloor$. Suppose some connected component of $G_0$ has line measure at most $\\Lambda\\epsilon$, where $\\Lambda\\ge1$. Define\n\n$$\nC_\\Lambda=\\sqrt{8{,}000{,}000\\,C_*\\Lambda}.\n$$\n\nIf $\\epsilon\\ge C_\\Lambda(d/p)^{1/2}$, then some total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial $Q$ satisfies\n\n$$\n\\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]\\ge\\frac{\\epsilon}{160000}.\n$$\n\nThis is conditional only in the campaign sense: the implication is proved, but the extra small-component premise is not known for every table.\n\n**Proposition C (proved global-cover obstruction).** Let $p\\ge103$ be prime and let $2\\le K\\le p/(100\\log p)$. For every campaign degree $100<d<p$, there is a valid table using constant line polynomials for which\n\n$$\n\\frac1{2K}\\le\\epsilon\\le\\frac2K,\n$$\n\nall accepted point and line degrees are within a factor two of their means, and every set $R$ which reaches at least half of the accepted points of every affine line satisfies\n\n$$\n|R|\\ge\\frac{Kp}{16}\\ge\\frac{p}{32\\epsilon}.\n$$\n\nNevertheless, one constant global polynomial agrees with $f$ on at least $\\epsilon/2$ of the points.\n\n## Proof or Conditional Proof\n\n**[P1] (proved: component size).** Apply KTZ Lemma 2.1 to the point and line sides of $G$. Its exact mixing error is $\\sqrt{\\mu(X)\\mu(T)}/\\sqrt{p+1}$. The first hypothesis and $d>100$ imply $1/\\sqrt{p+1}\\le k/(4p)$. The argument of KTZ Lemma 6.2 therefore gives\n\n$$\n\\mu(X),\\tau\\ge\\frac{k}{4p}.\n$$\n\nIn particular, $t\\ge k(p+1)/4$.\n\n**[P2] (proved: component-sensitive reaching family).** Select each line of $T$ independently with probability $8/k$. For $x\\in N_G(L)$, at least $k-1$ other accepted lines pass through $x$, so $x$ is reached with probability at least $3/4$. For distinct points of a fixed affine line $L$, the relevant sets of other lines are disjoint: two such points have only $L$ as a common line. The reach indicators are consequently independent. Chernoff gives failure probability at most $e^{-\\deg_G(L)/24}\\le e^{-k/24}$ for a fixed $L$. The hypotheses imply $k\\gg\\log p$, so a union bound over fewer than $2p^2$ lines succeeds. A second Chernoff bound controls the sample size. Hence one choice $R$ has, with $r=|R|$,\n\n$$\n\\frac{4t}{k}\\le r\\le\\frac{16t}{k},\n$$\n\nand reaches more than half of $N_G(L)$ for every $L\\in T$. By [P1], $r\\ge p+1$.\n\n**[P3] (proved: interpolation).** Set\n\n$$\nD=16\\bigl(\\lfloor\\sqrt{dr}\\rfloor+1\\bigr).\n$$\n\nThen $D^2>256dr$ and\n\n$$\nD^2\\le1024dr\\le\\frac{16384dt}{k}.\n$$\n\nUsing $t=\\tau p(p+1)\\le2\\tau p^2$ and $k^3\\ge C_*d\\tau p^2$ gives $D/k<1/5000$. Thus $D<k\\le p$, while $r\u0003e p>D$.\n\nKTZ Lemma 2.2 supplies at least $D^3/(64d)$ eligible monomials of weighted degree at most $D$. Each identity $A(L(t),P_L(t))\\equiv0$ costs at most $D+1$ homogeneous conditions, and $D^2>256dr$ makes the number of variables exceed $r(D+1)$. A nonzero interpolant exists. Because $r>D$, it cannot be independent of $Z$; the allowed $Z$-exponents make $A_Z\\ne0$. Choose one of minimum weighted degree. KTZ Lemma 2.3 makes it squarefree.\n\n**[P4] (proved: extension).** If $x$ is an accepted point of $L$ reached by $M\\in R\\setminus\\{L\\}$, then $P_L(x)=f(x)=P_M(x)$ and hence $A(x,P_L(x))=0$. More than $k/2>D$ distinct such points lie on every $L\\in T$. Since $A(L(t),P_L(t))$ has degree at most $D$, it vanishes identically. No Markov loss occurs here.\n\n**[P5] (proved: characteristic-safe cleanup).** Write $A=HB$, where $H(X,Y)$ is the gcd of the coefficients of $A$ as a polynomial in $Z$. At most $D$ lines make $H|_L$ identically zero. On every other line, exact division in the domain $\\mathbb F_p[t]$ gives $B(L(t),P_L(t))\\equiv0$. The squarefree $B$ has degree below $p$. KTZ Lemma 2.4 supplies a formal directional derivative $\\mathcal D$ with $\\gcd(B,\\mathcal DB)=1$. KTZ Lemma 2.6, via the resultant, bounds lines on whose polynomial graph both vanish by $D(D-1)$. Every other line has at most $D$ accepted points where $(\\mathcal DB)(x,f(x))=0$. Thus at most\n\n$$\npD^2+Dt\n$$\n\naccepted edges are deleted.\n\n**[P6] (proved: local deletion and pruning).** Since $|E|\\ge kt$,\n\n$$\n\\frac{pD^2+Dt}{|E|}\\le\\frac{D}{k}+\\frac{16384dp}{k^2}<\\frac1{4000}.\n$$\n\nPut $k_1=\\lfloor k/100\\rfloor$. Iteratively deleting vertices of current degree below $k_1$ removes fewer than\n\n$$\nk_1(|X|+t)\\le 2(k_1/k)|E|\\le |E|/50\n$$\n\nadditional edges. A nonempty graph of minimum degree $k_1$ remains, although it may fragment.\n\n**[P7] (proved: simple roots).** At every remaining point choose two incident lines; $k_1\\ge2$. Their directions are independent in $\\mathbb F_p^2$. Differentiating their formal identities, if $B_Z(x,f(x))=0$, forces $B_X(x,f(x))=B_Y(x,f(x))=0$. This contradicts the retained condition $(\\mathcal DB)(x,f(x))\\ne0$. Hence every retained value is a simple $Z$-root. No derivative is divided by in this step.\n\n**[P8] (proved: lift, propagate, and measure).** Choose any connected component of the graph left by [P6]. Its minimum degree is $k_1>D$. KTZ Lemmas 2.7 and 6.1 lift the line roots through one point to a single total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial $Q$. Simple-root uniqueness propagates the same $Q$ through the component, as in KTZ Lemma 6.3; there is no factor-count loss. The first numerical hypothesis also gives $1/\\sqrt{p+1}\\le k_1/(4p)$. KTZ Lemma 6.2 then gives point measure at least\n\n$$\n\\frac{k_1}{4p}\\ge\\frac{k}{800p},\n$$\n\nproving Theorem A.\n\n**[P9] (conditional: translate to local agreement).** In Corollary B, the stated threshold makes $k_0\\ge\\epsilon p/200$. The inequality $\\epsilon^2p\\ge8{,}000{,}000C_*\\Lambda d$ implies both\n\n$$\nk_0^2\\ge C_*dp,\n\\qquad\nk_0^3\\ge C_*d(\\Lambda\\epsilon)p^2.\n$$\n\nTheorem A therefore gives agreement at least $k_0/(800p)\\ge\\epsilon/160000$. This is the exponent-$1/2$ calculation. Its only unresolved campaign premise is the existence of the component with $\\tau\\le\\Lambda\\epsilon$.\n\n**[P10] (proved: realizable balanced-color graph).** Independently color every point and every affine line uniformly from $[K]$, inject the colors into $\\mathbb F_p$, put $f(x)$ equal to the point color, and make $P_L$ the corresponding constant line color. Conditional on a vertex color, its accepted degree is binomial with mean $p/K$ on the line side or $(p+1)/K$ on the point side. A Chernoff bound followed by a union bound over fewer than $3p^2$ vertices has failure probability at most $6p^2e^{-p/(12K)}<1$. Therefore a deterministic realization with the claimed degree bounds exists. These constant line polynomials are degree at most the fixed campaign degree $d>100$; this is not an analysis of the excluded case $d=0$.\n\n**[P11] (proved: cover lower bound).** Let $S$ be the points incident to an accepted edge from $R$. The line-degree upper bound gives $|S|\\le2p|R|/K$. If every line has at least half its accepted points in $S$, summing over all $p(p+1)$ lines gives at least $p^2(p+1)/(4K)$ accepted incidences from $S$. The point-degree upper bound is $2(p+1)/K$, so $|S|\\ge p^2/8$. Hence $|R|\\ge Kp/16$. Since $\\epsilon\\ge1/(2K)$, this is at least $p/(32\\epsilon)$. Finally, $f$ takes only $K$ values, so one constant has agreement at least $1/K\\ge\\epsilon/2$.\n\n## Exponent Ledger\n\n| Stage | Input | Output/loss |\n|---|---|---|\n| KTZ reconstruction | $|R|=O(p/\\epsilon)$ | $D=O(\\sqrt{dp/\\epsilon})$; $D<\\epsilon p$ gives $\\epsilon^3\\gtrsim d/p$ |\n| Component sampling | $t=\\tau p(p+1)$, degree $k$ | $|R|=\\Theta(t/k)=\\Theta(\\tau p^2/k)$; Chernoff/union losses are constant |\n| Interpolation | $r=|R|$ | $D=\\Theta(\\sqrt{dr})=O(\\sqrt{d\\tau p^2/k})$ |\n| Extension | root supply $k$ | $D<k$ requires $k^3\\gtrsim d\\tau p^2$ |\n| Degenerate deletion | bad edges $pD^2+Dt$ | relative loss $O(dp/k^2+D/k)$, requiring $k^2\\gtrsim dp$ |\n| Mixing | minimum degree $k_1$ | $p^{-1/2}\\lesssim k_1/p$; absorbed because $d>100$ and $k^2\\gg dp$ |\n| Global agreement | surviving component | $\\Pr[Q=f]\\ge k/(800p)$; no power loss |\n| Small-component corollary | $k=\\Theta(\\epsilon p)$, $\\tau=O(\\epsilon)$ | both constraints become $\\epsilon^2\\gtrsim d/p$ |\n| Global-cover obstruction | $\\epsilon=\\Theta(1/K)$ | every uniform cover has $|R|=\\Omega(Kp)=\\Omega(p/\\epsilon)$ |\n\n## Counterexample Attempts\n\nThe balanced-color table proves that the $p/\\epsilon$ global-cover scale is not merely an artifact of independent sampling. It is an actual degree-at-most-$d$ line-versus-point table. It does not refute soundness, because a constant decoder already has agreement $\\Omega(\\epsilon)$.\n\nConcentrating all good lines in a subset of directions also does not refute the conditional result. With at least two good directions, the accepted graph is connected, has line measure $\\tau=\\epsilon$, and is governed by the planted polynomial.\n\nIndependent random tables accept with probability about $1/p$, far below the campaign range. Attempts using $t^p-t$ or other inseparable functional aliases require line degree at least $p$ and are excluded by $d<p$.\n\nAt the lower boundary $d=101$, the square-root scale decreases from about $0.9902$ at $p=103$ to $0.1005$ at $p=10007$. When $d$ approaches $p$, both cube-root and square-root scales approach one, and the large-constant hypotheses become vacuous. No near-$p$ counterexample is claimed.\n\n## Characteristic Audit\n\nThe proof is entirely over the prime field and keeps $D<p$. Formal and functional identities cannot be confused for line polynomials because $d<p$. The eligible-monomial restriction ensures $A_Z\\ne0$. Squarefreeness and perfectness rule out an inseparable irreducible factor with all partial derivatives zero. The generic derivative is chosen over $\\mathbb F_p$ using fewer than $p$ exceptional hyperplanes. Resultants, rather than discriminant division, control common line roots. Newton lifting divides only by a certified nonzero simple-root derivative. Ordinary interpolation is used with no hidden multiplicities or Hasse-derivative assumptions.\n\n## Limitations\n\nThe result does not prove that every accepted core contains a component with $\\tau=O(\\epsilon)$. A single connected component may have $\\tau$ close to one, in which case the new parameter law returns the cubic benchmark. The explicit constant $2^{40}$ is safe but intentionally very loose, making the finite nonvacuous range much smaller than the exponent notation suggests. Proposition C obstructs only a single cover required to work for every line; it does not obstruct an algebraically informed decoder or an adaptive localization argument. Finite computations above are falsification checks only and play no role in the asymptotic proof.","obstructions":["The existence of a pruned accepted component with line measure tau=O(epsilon) is not implied by local agreement in the present proof.","Balanced constant-label tables force Omega(p/epsilon) lines for any single family covering half of every accepted line, so a uniformly smaller purely graph-theoretic replacement for KTZ Lemma 3.2 is false.","The component theorem has an independent k^2=Omega(dp) cleanup requirement, imposing a square-root floor even if tau is much smaller.","For d/p larger than the reciprocal of the large absolute constant, the conditional theorem is vacuous; in particular it gives no information when d is close to p."],"parameter_regime":"p prime; 100<d<p; m=2.
